# Skills · A · part 36

778 entries, adversarial-review to advisor-intake. [Index](README.md) · [Everything](../README.md)

- **adversarial-review**: Lean agent's critic as a skill. Reviews one change (a pull request, a branch, or an agent's working tree) as an adversary: gates on what the diff touches, has one fresh agent hunt for defects against fixed questions and the repository's own rules, has a second fresh agent try to disprove every finding, and reports only what survives, with proof. Routes findings back to the agent that wrote the change, or posts them as review threads. Use it on every agent-made change before it merges, and whenever asked for an adversarial, critic or pre-merge review. ([arnelirobles/lean-agent](https://github.com/arnelirobles/lean-agent/tree/HEAD/skills/adversarial-review))
- **adversarial-review**: Stress-tests code, plans, ideas, architecture, requirements, policies, research, and documents using evidence-gated critique and optional external research. Use when the user explicitly asks for an adversarial review, red-team review, stress test, skeptical critique, or challenge of an artifact or decision. ([Autopsias/gearbox-routing](https://github.com/Autopsias/gearbox-routing/tree/HEAD/harness/skills/adversarial-review/codex))
- **adversarial-review**: Find concrete defects in a material code change, architecture decision, security boundary, or completion claim. Do not turn a trivial edit into a large review ritual. - **adversarial-review**: Human-in-the-loop adversarial review between Kimi Code CLI and the other roster's CLIs (Claude Code by default, Codex for a screen or an asset, the Antigravity CLI as a second opinion). The interactive session agent authors (plan or diff) collaboratively with the user; a different vendor's CLI is invoked headlessly as the adversarial reviewer. Modes - plan (draft and defend an implementation plan) and code (review the working diff). - **adversarial-review**: Use after implementing increments and before verification or merge — to review the full diff adversarially: assume it is wrong and try to prove it. Challenges the assumptions the change rests on (including how the criteria were read), hunts implementation gaps (unmet criteria, untried edge cases, unhandled failure paths, detached work nobody owns), hunts test gaps (plausible bugs no test would catch, tests that can't fail), and flags complexity that could be deleted with every criterion still met. Trigger whenever work is about to be called done — a feature, a bugfix, and just as much a small… ([bryceklinker/claude-skills](https://github.com/bryceklinker/claude-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/adversarial-review))

- **adversarial-review**: Run the hardening phase for one increment as a single entry point: spec what must be protected against, drive the code to green, and gate it. Sequences /test-spec (S5) → /finish-build (S6) → /acceptance-gate (S7), so the owner meets one skill instead of three. Its subject is what the design NEVER DECIDED — failure modes, edge cases, boundaries the spec named but did not cross, and the silly errors that survive a spec-conformant build. It does not re-check the code against the design doc; `conformance` did that at S3, and conformance and completeness are orthogonal.
